STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/L/Leitch_Barry/
> COMMENT: All Barry's comments are indicated by (BL).
>
> TITLE: Hoodoo Voodoo
>  ARTIST: Graham Marsh (BOGG)
> COMMENT: "Icups - my first published work... I had written the tune as a demo,
>          and I had been trading with Bogg for a few years, and I had re-written
>          his tune just to see how I would have taken his initial idea and run
>          with it myself. Anyways, I had sent disks with a load of tunes on to
>          all the major software companies at the time. Firebird contacted me,
>          they wanted to use Icups. I told them at the time that it was kind of
>          a rip off of Bogg's Detergent, but they were quite happy to offer to
>          buy it. They offered me 150 pounds, and then when someone pointed out
>          again to them that it was based on Bogg's tune, they gave him half the
>          money too which I was quite happy with." (BL)

>          workdisks.

>          for doing so much work, but they all agreed mine was better ! :)

>          guy" = Anthony Lees)

## Air on a Rasterline

Path: `MUSICIANS/L/Lft/Air_on_a_Rasterline.sid`

Author: Linus Åkesson (lft) · Release/year: 2013 lft

PSID; BASIC flag clear; 1 subtunes. Complete-file MD5: `dcc9d4ca2677b339ebb0b3adccbbb2cc`; SHA-256: `c4361fc538e42ac564eb2d31082630673d526ea9d33b3725e2ccd71947118e22`.

| Subtune | Seconds | Evidence | Reference RMS; writes | Neither | K-only | B-only |
|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2 | pcm-difference-with-similar-write-sequence | 2.713e-2; 234 | ΔRMS 3.832e-5; writes 234; prefix 234; drift 13; derived | identical PCM; writes 234; prefix 234; drift 0; derived | ΔRMS 3.832e-5; writes 234; prefix 234; drift 13 |

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/L/Lft/
> COMMENT: Lft's (Linus Akesson) own comments are denoted (LA)
>
> COMMENT: "The tune is remarkable from a technical point of view, because the
>          playroutine only needs 63 clock cycles (one rasterline) per frame,
>          whereas a normal playroutine needs around 24 rasterlines. To put that
>          in perspective, consider that simply calling the playroutine (jump to
>          subroutine + return from subroutine) uses up 12 cycles, i.e. almost 20%
>          of the available time. Obviously this severely limits the number of
>          features the player can support; there's no vibrato, no arpeggios, and
>          so on. Still, it was fun to try to work musically within those extreme
>          constraints.
>          This project then went on to inspire Hermit Soft to create One
>          Rasterline Tracker, a tool that lets non-coders take part in the fun.
>          However, his playroutine is not quite down to 63 cycles yet, and
>          believe me, the last few are the hardest. Still, it's a great
>          achievement!" (LA)

## Allt under himmelens fäste

Path: `MUSICIANS/L/Lft/Allt_under_himmelens_faeste.sid`

Author: Linus Åkesson (lft) · Release/year: 2007 lft

PSID; BASIC flag clear; 1 subtunes. Complete-file MD5: `27d4f0200628a23c8089088e34db8f7b`; SHA-256: `81e97582af3b6da2feb38f5798c75386443ac2482bd27969094fd144e3750219`.

| Subtune | Seconds | Evidence | Reference RMS; writes | Neither | K-only | B-only |
|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2 | pcm-difference-with-similar-write-sequence | 5.924e-2; 588 | ΔRMS 2.916e-3; writes 588; prefix 588; drift 54; derived | identical PCM; writes 588; prefix 588; drift 0; derived | ΔRMS 2.916e-3; writes 588; prefix 588; drift 54 |

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/L/Lft/
> COMMENT: Lft's (Linus Akesson) own comments are denoted (LA)
>
> TITLE: Allt under himmelens fäste
>  ARTIST: Visa Röster
> COMMENT: "At a concert where Visa Röster interpreted both SID tunes and Swedish
>          folk songs, my girlfriend and I bought a couple of CDs, and found a
>          little gem called Allt under himmelens fäste that we both instantly
>          liked. It's a very sad song with a simple melody, supported by a
>          solemn yet rhythmical accompaniment written by Mahoney. If you're a
>          SID composer, you know that when you get a song stuck on your mind,
>          it inevitably ends up as a SID tune. =) In this case, I've tried to
>          stick to Mahoney's arrangement as far as possible." (LA)

## Specular Highlight

Path: `MUSICIANS/L/Lft/Specular_Highlight.sid`

Author: Linus Åkesson (lft) · Release/year: 2014 lft

PSID; BASIC flag clear; 1 subtunes. Complete-file MD5: `86de0485114d0f746b5ef59f31442967`; SHA-256: `1b609be42b0332036c63cfb71367e4345f92cfd39c24a75056698f24ada82b5a`.

| Subtune | Seconds | Evidence | Reference RMS; writes | Neither | K-only | B-only |
|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2 | pcm-difference-with-similar-write-sequence | 1.375e-1; 2449 | ΔRMS 7.151e-2; writes 2449; prefix 2449; drift 13; derived | identical PCM; writes 2449; prefix 2449; drift 0; derived | ΔRMS 7.151e-2; writes 2449; prefix 2449; drift 13 |

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/L/Lft/
> COMMENT: Lft's (Linus Akesson) own comments are denoted (LA)
>
> COMMENT: "I composed this SID tune for the Executable Music Oldskool compo at
>          Revision 2014, where it ended up on 5th place.
>          This piece is a genre study: It is heavily inspired by the kind of
>          lyrical and highly energetic J-pop that tends to be used as opening
>          themes for Anime series. During the (anonymised) compo some people
>          speculated that this was a cover song. This is nice to hear, because it
>          means that I managed to capture the lyrical style well. My conclusion
>          from the experiment is that the genre works well with 8-bit sounds."
>          (LA)
